Mothman — Campaign Progress & Match Videos (Loops 29–42) #

Note

Where things stand: Our fifth live release on the public AI Arena ladder (Loop 42) just deployed after our strongest local test cycle so far. Before the handover, Loop 37 closed its ladder run at 23–11 (67.6% wins), and Loop 40 stands at 6–9–1 (29–20 combined across the two builds), with Terran climbing to a new personal-best 1267 rating and Mothman-X beating Cortex on the back of late-game Brood Lords.

Loop 42 combines two cycles of improvements into one release. Against our fourteen regular sparring bots, it broke past our long-standing 18-win ceiling to finish 20–21 with an even 1.00× trade ratio—our first build to trade resource-for-resource across the full core roster—and reached 30–50–3 (0.77× trade) across all 84 practice matches while clearing six recurring failure modes to zero. In our six-match head-to-head gate against the live Loop 40 bots, Loop 42 won 5–1 (2.12× trade, with Protoss going 3–0) and passed its four-race standalone package check 4–0. Starting with Loop 42, every single match (90 full-length games) and tactical drill (116 battles) is also recorded as an H.264 MP4 video with a live StarCraft II spectator HUD.

Campaign Snapshot #

Live Ladder (AI Arena)Core Practice RosterOverall Local PracticeHead-to-Head vs. Loop 40Full Match Videos
Loop 42 deployed live · Loop 37 finished 23–11 (67.6%), Loop 40 at 6–9–1 (29–20 combined)20–21 (48.8% wins, 1.00× trade) · four 3–0 sweeps30–50–3 (36.1% wins, 0.77× trade, 4.0% supply-blocked)5–1 (2.12× trade; Protoss 3–0) · 4–0 standalone package check214 H.264 MP4 videos (90 full matches, 116 drills, 8 extended highlights)
Quick guide to terms
  • Trade ratio (army value traded): Total mineral and gas value of enemy units destroyed divided by what our bot lost. Above 1.0× means the bot comes out ahead in fights.
  • Supply-blocked: Share of the game where the bot could not build units because it hit its current supply cap (Depot, Pylon, or Overlord). Lower is better; under 4% is steady.
  • Core vs. unseen opponents: Core bots are 14 regular sparring partners; unseen bots are 12 held-out bots we avoid tuning against specifically, checking that changes generalize.
  • Head-to-head ship gate: Before deploying a new build to AI Arena, it plays six matches against the currently live version across all matchups.
  • Tactics Arena: Short StarCraft II combat and mineral-defense drills rendered directly inside the real StarCraft II 3D engine.

Every video and still frame in this report is captured directly from the real StarCraft II 3D game engine. Earlier loops (19–28) live in the Phase 1 archive, and the filterable library of full-length games and combat drills is in Replays & Drills.

Loop 41 — Live Ladder Progress, Planetary Fortress Drill Win, and Unseen Practice Highs #

Summary #

  • What changed: Dark Templars now wait to blink onto a fortified Planetary Fortress as one coordinated group; Zerg Queens and Drones prioritize defending early Reaper raids without stalling mining; Terran SCVs no longer interrupt combat to repair during early worker rushes; and Protoss Observers stay closer to home while Terran protects its first Factory add-on.
  • How the bots performed: On the live AI Arena ladder, our newly deployed Loop 40 bots opened 5–4 with a 1.33× trade ratio (destroying 33% more army value than lost), beating 1631-rated Thssprtssbt (7.35×, after 0–12) and shutting out FastBC (43.50×, zero units lost). All four live bots reached new high ratings (1286, 1272, 1266, and 1239). Locally, Loop 41 reached 7 wins against the unseen practice roster, destroyed the Planetary Fortress in the Dark Templar drill, and defended Reaper harassment at 97% mining uptime.
  • What we are fixing next (Loop 42): Because Loop 41 lost 2–4 in head-to-head testing against our live Loop 40 bots, we held deploying for one cycle to combine Loop 41 and Loop 42. Loop 42 focuses on stopping early worker rushes across all three races, adding anti-air defense against cloaked Banshees, keeping Terran Siege Tank and Protoss Colossus production on schedule, and publishing full-length match videos with a replay scoreboard overlay.

Loop 42 — Live Release #5, Breaking the 20-Win Core Ceiling, and Full-Length Match Videos #

We held Loop 41 back from the live ladder for one cycle because it fell short (2–4) in head-to-head testing against our live Loop 40 bots. Watching those four losses closely gave us a clear checklist: Protoss was trickling Stalkers into Terran siege lines while starving its own first Colossus, Terran was draining its gas on early Vikings before building four Siege Tanks, and all three races were still losing to fast cloaked Banshees or stalling on cramped building placement.

Loop 42 addresses each of those problems directly and pairs them with a complete overhaul of how we record and present our matches. Instead of short animated GIFs, every single game and tactical drill is now rendered as a seekable H.264 MP4 video with a live StarCraft II spectator HUD showing player names, team colors, worker counts, supply, and the game clock.

What changed and why it mattered #

The largest jump came from mid-game discipline. Protoss now reserves the gas and minerals needed for its first two Colossi once the Robotics Bay finishes and holds back early ground attacks in Protoss-versus-Terran until its first Colossus is ready or its army reaches critical mass. In Protoss mirrors, it also protects steady Immortal production before drifting into extra tech. The result was immediate: in head-to-head testing against the live Loop 40 bots, Protoss went 3–0 with a 3.49× trade ratio and zero logged faults—turning our troublesome Protoss-versus-Terran game from a 0.25× loss into a clean 2.38× win.

Against cloaked Banshees, we unified our response across all three races as soon as a Banshee or early Tech-Lab Starport is scouted: Terran puts up Missile Turrets and four Vikings, Zerg expands its Queen count alongside Spores and early Hydralisks, and Protoss boosts Stalkers and Phoenixes while keeping Observers tethered near home. We also widened building-placement search when a Terran Barracks or Protoss Gateway gets blocked in a crowded base, added a late natural-expansion safeguard so bots stop getting stuck on one base, and restored Zerg Mutalisk magic-box spacing down to the last two surviving Mutalisks against Thors.

How the bots performed #

Across our 14 core sparring partners, Loop 42 broke through the 18-win ceiling that had held since Loop 32, finishing 20–21 (48.8% wins) and posting our first even 1.00× trade ratio on the core roster, with clean 3–0 sweeps of titania, monsterbot, puck, and onlyfans. Across all 84 local practice matches, the build finished 30–50–3 (0.77× trade, up from 0.68× in Loop 41) and cleared six recurring failure categories to zero in the same run: cloaked-unit losses, one-base lockups, dropped Spires, air-defense starvation, Protoss natural delays, and idle mineral banks. Production placement stalls fell from five down to one. Terran also earned its first win over held-out opponent Mulebot (4.49× trade), meaning all three races have now beaten it.

In the six-match head-to-head ship gate against our live Loop 40 packages, Loop 42 won 5–1 with a 2.12× aggregate trade ratio (3.09× median) and beat Loop 40 on every macro check. It then swept its four-race standalone package smoke test 4–0 (7.72× trade, with all four winning replays carrying the live build tag), clearing it for our fifth live deployment to AI Arena.

On the live ladder itself, Loop 37 closed out its final tally at 23–11 (67.6% wins), and Loop 40 stands at 6–9–1 (29–20 combined across the two builds). Recent live highlights include Mothman-X rolling Zerg and wearing down Cortex with late-game Brood Lords, and Mothman-T climbing to a personal-best 1267 rating while trading at 1.41× with under 1% time supply-blocked. North-versus-south spawn balance remains steady both live (40.0% north vs. 33.3% south) and across 467 local games since our symmetry fix (exact 4.1% supply-blocked on both halves of the map).

6. Self-play Probe mineral defense — e01 (PvP · Pass · 95.7% uptime) #

Defending Probes eliminate both raiding Probes while losing only one worker and sustaining 95.7% mining uptime across the full drill. (Still frame)

What still needs work (Loop 43) #

Four clear gaps stood out in our Loop 42 review and are already underway in Loop 43. First, early all-in worker rushes against fast Probe micro still punish our workers when melee swings spread across healthy targets instead of finishing off low-HP Probes. Second, in the Dark Templar drill (and generally whenever cloaked or burrowed units fight near static detection), units should clear Missile Turrets, Cannons, or Spores first so they can fight while invisible. Third, Terran occasionally still lets enemy Overlords trigger unnecessary Viking production against ground Zerg armies and overbuilds Medivacs in Terran mirrors. Finally, we are expanding the Tactics Arena from 58 scenarios to more than 200 drills—adding Stalker blink-back and blink-snipe, Widow Mine drops and defense, worker runbys, Oracle Stasis Wards, and transport micro.
